### UI textures by name: where a texture swap per culture can go (2026-11-27)

For the paused "Culture UI" request: can one HUD take other textures per culture? REA (Ghidra 12.1.4, snapshot
`rea/snapshots/age3y-b1a1dab7.json`), Evidence bundle `rea/evidence/age3y-b1a1dab7-culture-ui-20261127.json`
(IDs shortened to 8 hex digits), capstone (`cs`) of the exe's bytes, and a hardware-breakpoint trace of the running game
in `C:\AoE3-UHC-WoLReal6` (WoL 1.0.15c data, our DLL, two scenario games in one launch; log, tracer and captures in
`rea/evidence/culture-ui-20261127/`). [O] observed, [I] inferred, [U] unknown. Report and verdict:
[wol-fixes/culture-ui/feasibility-20261127.md](../wol-fixes/culture-ui/feasibility-20261127.md).<!-- private -->

| What | Where | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Layout `background` | FUN_00629466, one of the two readers of the string 0xBA6AA8 (the other is 0x62AD29) | confirmed (`ev_dd7d33a0`, `ev_fd5c6f52`) | [O] `background` → 0x638823(gadget, &path); else `backgroundColor` → a colour drawable (0x5C1CAF). Then `useColorTransform`, `clampBackground`, `clickThruFile`. |
| `stateEntry` background | FUN_0062AC80 → 0x62A6DC | confirmed (`ev_fd5c6f52`, `cs`) | [O] Each `stateEntry`'s `background` → 0x62A6DC: a new drawable (`[[0xC66244]+44h]` vtable `+14h`(1)) and its vtable `+7Ch`(path, −1), the same call as 0x638823. |
| UI drawable: texture by path | 0x638883 = vtable 0xB988B0 `+7Ch`, `thiscall(drawable, BString* path, int)`, `ret 8` | confirmed (`cs`) | [O] Copies the path (0x443AA0), then **`call 58E3EBh` at 0x6388FE** with the texture manager `[0xC66044]+6A8h` and `(path, 0, 0, −1)`, then `+78h`(handle, arg2) (0x445060, the texture by handle). [I] The drawable 0x638823 and 0x62A6DC make is this class (vtable 0xB988B0 is the only one holding 0x638883). |
| Texture handle by name | 0x58E3EB, `thiscall(texMgr, const wchar_t* path, bool, int, int)`, `ret 10h` | confirmed (`ev_498b5b56`, `cs`) | [O] Head `51 56 8B 74 24 0C 85 F6 57 8B F9` (`push ecx / push esi / mov esi,[esp+0Ch] / test esi,esi / push edi / mov edi,ecx`). Looks the path up in the manager's name table `[mgr+10h]` (0x467E4C hash, 0x467D12 find): found → its handle. Else 0x469479(mgr, path, arg3, 0) (block 0x59AD18). Key = the path as given (a BString copy, 0x467DFC): no case folding or extension seen. 110 resolved callers plus 30 unresolved (`ev_2ec2d581`): every texture of the game, not only the UI. |
| New texture entry | 0x469479, `thiscall(texMgr, const wchar_t* name, int, int)` | confirmed (`cs`) | [O] Tries `<name>.amt` and `<name>.amt.xmb` (strings 0xB9EFC0, 0xB9EFA8) in two folders through the file manager 0xD6F3D8 (0x46930E, the "file exists" test [I]); none → a 30h-byte entry (0x46942B), the name at `+4`, appended to `[mgr+4]` and added to the name table (0x469388). **No texture file is read here**: [I] the data loads at first use, as the 2026-11-25 memory test measured. |
| When the HUD resolves its names | FUN_0076166A (HUD build), from FUN_0076375C (the game screen's start, its only caller, `ev_4dbaf654`, `ev_65327cb1`) | confirmed in game (trace) | [O] Game 1: the HUD build ran 32.8 s into the launch, after the world existed, with the local player (1) and its civ (Ethiopians) readable; the 0x638823 calls for every HUD gadget and the first 0x469479 of `ui\ingame\nat_ui_max`, `asi_ui_max`, `eur_max_ui`, `ingame_ui_small_01_ypack`, ... followed in the same 0.3 s. The resource panel chooser 0x454F2F ran right after (and again from 0x4559AE at each refresh). The scenario editor before it did not run the HUD build. |
| A second game in the same launch | the same | confirmed in game (trace, captures) | [O] Game 2: the HUD build ran again and every HUD gadget called 0x638823 again (**the HUD is rebuilt each game**), but no `ui\ingame` name reached 0x469479: the manager kept the entries by name. [O] `ingame_ui_small_01_ypack.ddt` replaced on disk (loose, recoloured) during game 1: game 1 and game 2 still showed the old art; after a restart the new art showed. So **texture data is cached by name for the whole launch**: replacing or redirecting the file itself between games does nothing; giving the gadget **another name** (`x_<suffix>`) makes a new entry, loaded from its own file. [I] Each culture played in one launch keeps its textures until exit (at most the 7.8 MB of the HUD's `ui\ingame` set, only what was drawn). |
| Texture coordinates | `textureCoords` (string 0xBA3778) in FUN_00629A7A → gadget vtable `+D8h` = 0x44A1E9 for a plain gadget (`button` 0xAB7F71 calls it too) | confirmed (`ev_18d1be98`, `cs`) | [O] 0x44A1E9 forwards the four floats to the drawable `[gadget+58h]` vtable `+B0h`; the gadget keeps no copy. [O] 0x638823 makes a new drawable and 0x593B58 frees the old one. So **a texture set after the build (0x638823) loses the layout's `textureCoords`** unless the caller sets them again (vtable `+D8h`), as `politicianEnablesTech` does. 0x593B58 re-derives the drawable's flags (tiling, clamping) from the gadget's own bits `[+0A5h]`, `[+0AAh]`: [I] those survive. |
| Observer, player view | — | partly [U] | [O] The stock exe has no observer mode (no string; `ev_1d0c6b0f`). [O] WoL's observers are real players in the last slots with a civ of their own, and WoL's DLL loads `uimainnewobserver<res>.xml`, whose atlases are others (`ingame_ui_01x_ypack`, `ingame_ui_01x_asian`, `ingame_ui_05`, `ingame_ui_06`; [observer-mode.md](observer-mode.md)). [U] Whether the replay panel's player switch (0x75E9A7) changes `[game+3C0h]` or rebuilds the HUD: not read. [I] The HUD's names are fixed at its build, so a style would not follow a view switch inside a game. |

Where a key would redirect (design for B1, nothing written):

- [I] **The head of 0x58E3EB** (6 bytes `push ecx / push esi / mov esi,[esp+0Ch]`, re-executed by the hook): when a
  game world exists and the path starts with `ui\ingame\`, swap the argument `[esp+4]` for `<path>_<suffix>` if that
  texture exists (the game's own test of a file, as 0x469479 does, or a list read at startup). It covers every UI
  path at once (layout backgrounds, `stateEntry`s, `foreground`s, the pie chart's `texture`, code that loads by name)
  and both interfaces, every resolution, the observer files. Risk: it is on every texture lookup of the game, so the
  filter must be cheap (a prefix test, then a cache of decisions) and the key A/B-tested with `perfprobe.py`.
- [I] Narrower: **the call 0x6388FE** inside the UI drawable's texture-by-path: only gadget textures set by path
  (layout `background`, `stateEntry`s, 0x638823 from code). It misses anything that loads by name elsewhere.
- [I] Not recommended: 0x638823 on each HUD gadget after the build: it loses `textureCoords` (above), and the HUD has
  about 900 gadgets per interface.

### Culture UI: the style switch during a game (K0, 2026-11-26)

For the `cultureUI` key: how to switch the HUD's textures at the Great War, mid-game.<!-- private -->
REA (Ghidra 12.1.4, snapshot `rea/snapshots/age3y-b1a1dab7.json`), Evidence bundle
`rea/evidence/age3y-b1a1dab7-culture-ui-k0-20261126.json` (IDs shortened to 8 hex digits), capstone (`cs`) of the
exe's bytes, and a throw-away experiment build in `C:\AoE3-UHC-WoLReal6` (WoL 1.0.15c data, minimised UI, scenario
route; log, captures and the experiment's source in `rea/evidence/culture-ui-k0-20261126/`, not in git; picture
`_share\maston-20261128\K\K0_midgame_switch_test.png`). [O] observed, [I] inferred, [U] unknown.

| What | Where | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| UI drawable: texture by handle | 0x445060 = vtable 0xB988B0 `+78h`, `thiscall(drawable, handle, arg2)`, `ret 8` | confirmed (`cs`), in game | [O] Writes `[d+0A8h]` = handle, `[d+0ACh]` = arg2, then `+94h` (0x445039), which derives default coordinates only when `[d+8]` bit 2 is clear. The layout's `textureCoords` live in a 16-byte block `[d+0C0h]` that `+B0h` (0x44A203) fills, setting `[d+8]` bit 2. **So `+78h` (and `+7Ch`, which ends in it) on an existing drawable keeps its coordinates**; only 0x638823, which makes a new drawable, loses them. At the call 0x6388FE ESI is the drawable. |
| UI drawable: draw | 0x41E0E5 = vtable 0xB988B0 `+18h` | confirmed (`cs`) | [O] Each draw binds `[d+0A8h]` through 0x407D27 (texture manager, stage 0) at 0x41E1D8, then reads the coordinates `[d+0C0h]`. The handle is resolved again at every draw. |
| Texture bind | 0x407D27, `bool thiscall(texMgr, stage, handle)`, `ret 8`; head `55 8B 6C 24 08 56 8B F1` (8 B) | confirmed (`ev_ff26ae03`, `cs`), in game | [O] Entry `[mgr+4][handle]`; its vtable `+4` loads it if needed; vtable `+10h` (0x409984, `[entry+1Ch]`) gives the handle actually bound, and when that differs the other entry is loaded instead (an alias, used by `.amt` material entries [I]). Stage cache `[mgr+30A0h+4*stage]`; `SetTexture` through the device's vtable `+104h`. Above 80,000,000 bytes of texture memory (`[mgr+30E4h]`) the LRU `[mgr+3104h]` unloads (0x6C509C). 56 call sites: every textured draw of the game (terrain, models, UI). The only reference to the `+10h` thunk 0x409982 is this bind (`ev_ce5f0041`). |
| Texture entry | 30h bytes, vtable 0xB8566C, ctor 0x46942B | confirmed (`cs`) | [O] `+4` the name, `+1Ch` its own index, `+2Ch` loaded. `[entry+1Ch]` is also read by the destructor 0x588EC8 and the unload 0x6022FC (both 0x6022A5 by index) and after a load (0x598653 → 0x59839C by index: memory count and LRU). [I] So writing another index into `+1Ch` would make the bind follow it but would corrupt the memory count and the LRU: not usable. Swapping two pointers of `[mgr+4]` has the same LRU problem. |
| Missing texture file | 0x58E3EB → 0x469479 | confirmed (`cs`), in game | [O] 0x469479 tests only `<name>.amt` and `<name>.amt.xmb` (0x46930E); otherwise it **always** makes a new entry, without looking for the `.ddt`. In game, `ui\ingame\uhc_probe_missing_texture` got handle 11170 (entries 11170 → 11171), the same handle again on the second lookup. So 0x58E3EB returns −1 only for a null path or a failed allocation, not for a missing file. A key that renames must test the file itself. **Correction:** the comment at `src/features/fame.cpp` line 11 ("-1 ... when the art file is missing") is wrong on that point: a missing icon file still gets a handle (of an entry whose file is never found; what it draws was not checked); the key's behaviour is unchanged. |

[I] Needed only for a variant shipped inside a `.bar`; WoL ships its art loose (`art\ui\ingame\`, 172 files in the copy). |

So an engine rebuild of the whole HUD mid-game does not exist; toggling the option would also flash the other interface. |

In game: 0 before the build, 1 after it, option 49h = 1. Option table 0xD91778: 49h has flags 0x6005 (bool, in-match bit 40h set). |

In the test scenario: 0, local player 1 of 3 (gaia counted). **Re-read 2026-11-26 (REA on WoL's `uhc.dll` c070d138, bundle `rea/evidence/wol-uhc-c070d138-culture-ui-observer-20261126.json`, `ev_e97cf26f`, `ev_9aee89b3`):** [O] WoL's DLL does not read 37h for its observer interface: in FUN_10040C50 a local observer is "a trigger named `StartInsertion` exists" (0x1005BA00 → the exe's trigger lookup 0x90DFB0, cdecl(name), −1 if none) and (local player (0x96DB85, `[game+3C0h]`) ≥ the first observer (0x1005B9D0), or a state of its own == 2 (0x1005B9E0, [U] not identified)). The first observer (0x1005B710): with no such trigger, the player count `[world+5Ch]`; else 3 if team(2) ≠ team(1), 5 if team(3) ≠ team(2), 7 if team(4) ≠ team(3), else the player count; team(p) is the exe's 0x88396E, cdecl(player), `[player+10h]`, −2 without a world or player. `cultureUI` uses this rule (without the unidentified state). |

Gadget table `[[0xC66244]+44h]` `+68h` array, `+6Ch` count; a live slot `i` holds a gadget with `[g+0Ch] & 3FFFFh == i`. |

**The experiment** (two atlases, test copies recoloured with aoe3-blender-tools' `recolor_textures.py`; renamed at
the build and switched by a world tick at fixed game times):

- [O] **Rename at the build** (the head of 0x58E3EB, gated around the call 0x7637ED): the age box showed the red copy
  (`ingame_ui_02x_ypack` → `_k1`).
- [O] **Method B, remap at the bind** (the head of 0x407D27: when a table is active, the handle argument `[esp+8]` is
  replaced by `table[handle]`): at 15 s the minimap and command frames (stock name) turned red and the renamed age box
  turned blue (`_k1` → `_k2`), at once and in every gadget; at 30 s, table off, everything went back. No other change.
- [O] **Method A, `+78h` on every drawable** (walk of the gadget table, `[g+58h]` and the button state drawables):
  the same pixels as method B in this scene (4 and 7 drawables changed). But 60 of 2,685 live gadgets faulted when
  read this way (caught by SEH in the experiment), and it reaches only those two drawable fields: [I] foregrounds,
  progress bars and drawables set later by code would be missed, and a texture set by path after the switch gets the
  old name.
- [O] Method C (an engine rebuild): none exists for the whole HUD (row above).

[I] **Chosen for the key: method B for the whole job**, start and stage switch alike: one table handle → handle,
filled at the HUD build with every texture of the style that has a variant (the file test of row "Missing texture
file"), refilled when a stage's tech becomes active, emptied for a game without a style (observer, normal interface,
civ not in the table) and when the game ends. No lookup hook and no rename: a texture a gadget sets later by path
(the minimap filter button above) is remapped too, and a switch to the normal interface mid-game (0x760547) can empty
the table. Cost: one compare per texture bind while no table is active, a bounds test and a load while it is; to be
measured with `perfprobe.py`. Effect: a texture with a variant is replaced wherever it is drawn during the game,
in-game dialogs included (only shared textures such as `button` would show that).

### WoL requests #70–#73 (research for batch 2, 2026-11-20)

REA 6.8 (Ghidra 12.1.4) on the stock exe, snapshot `rea/snapshots/age3y-b1a1dab7-rea68.json`, bundle
`rea/evidence/wol-20261120/bundle.json`; capstone (`cs`) of the exe's bytes; hardware-breakpoint traces
(`tools/re/trace_exec.py`, `trace_writes.py`; logs in `rea/evidence/wol-20261120/`); in game in `C:\AoE3-UHC-WoLReal`
(scenario route and one Dutch skirmish). Evidence IDs are shortened to 8 hex digits. [O] observed, [I] inferred,
[U] unknown. The design built on these rows is [design-20261120.md](design-20261120.md).

### Particle emitter cycle (WoL #69, the works' smoke on and off, 2026-11-21)

REA 6.8 (Ghidra 12.1.4) on the stock exe, opened with snapshot `rea/snapshots/age3y-b1a1dab7-rea68.json`, and capstone
(`cs`) of the exe's bytes; checked in game in `C:\AoE3-UHC-WoLReal5`. [O] observed, [I] inferred, [U] unknown.

| Name | Where | Evidence | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `.particle` `<emitter>` parser | 0x59FF31 (reads at 0x5A11E3..) | `ev_8f96b5f7` | [O] Bytes: TiedToEmitter +3Ch, IgnoreRotation +3Dh, EmitByMotion +3Eh, Loop +3Fh, InheritVelocity +40h, UseMin/MaxVelocity +41h/+42h, AlwaysActive +43h, SyncWithAttackAnim +44h, CastShadows +45h. Floats: UpdateRadius +50h (clamped 3..25), ParticleLife/Var +58h/+5Ch, EmissionRate/Var +78h/+7Ch, InitialDormancy/Var +80h/+84h, InitialUpdate/Var +88h/+8Ch, EmissionTime/Var +90h/+94h, DormantTime/Var +98h/+9Ch (offsets in the emitter definition). |
| Varied value | 0x422929 | `cs` | [O] `cdecl(value, var)`: `value` when `var` <= 1e-6, else `value * (1 + var * r)`, `r` from 0x422648 (`thiscall(0xC66588, stream 4, -1.0, 1.0)`: an LCG per stream, `x = x * 0x343FD + 0x269EC3`, state advanced on every call). So a `*var` is a share of the value, and every call draws a new number. [U] Whether stream 4 is ever synchronised between players (not needed: visual only). |
| Emitter start | 0x5925B2 | `cs` | [O] Instance `+80h` = definition, `+5Ch` = state (1 dormant, 2 emitting, 3 done), `[+ECh]+4` = ms left in the state. Start time set back by `varied(InitialUpdate)` x 1000 ms (the pre-run). When `x + InitialDormancy` > 0 (0x592686..0x5926A2, `x` from 0x58EC4B, [U]) it starts dormant for `varied(InitialDormancy)` (0x592A58..), else emitting for `varied(EmissionTime)` (0x5926A8). |
| Emitter state machine | 0x425335 with pieces at 0x59309B, 0x4A6EA9, 0x5118C8, 0x6E5A2E | `cs` | [O] Runs only when `EmissionTime` > 1e-6, or `Loop` and `DormantTime` < 1e-6 (then it emits without end: the old `works_smoke`); else the emitter ends (state 3) unless EmitByMotion. Emitting runs out: dormant for `varied(DormantTime)`; with `Loop` 0 it ends. Dormant runs out: emitting for `varied(EmissionTime)`. Each switch draws again, per emitter instance. |
| AlwaysActive readers | 0x44E11D, 0xA21AE6 (`cmp byte [x+43h],0`) | `cs` | [U] Not traced. 0x44E11D skips a check through `[0xC67314]` vtable `+6Ch` when the flag is set. In game (2026-11-20) the arsenal's `white_smoke_b` with AlwaysActive 0 gave the age 2 works no smoke for a minute. |

In game (2026-11-21, `wol-fixes/paraguayan-works/LEEME-README.txt`, "Smoke on and off"): with EmissionTime 14 var 0.43 and
DormantTime 18.5 var 0.35, every chimney alternates on its own and no two are in step [O]; the double attach of the smoke
(component and Idle anim) makes one emitter per bone [I, from the measured pauses]. Since 2026-11-22 DormantTime is 21 (var
0.35, pauses 13.7-28.4 s, mean 21 = 1.5 x the mean puff, WoL #69): one chimney measured over 428 s of game time puffed
13 times for 8-23 s (mean 13.9) and paused 12 times for 13-26 s (mean 20.1) [O], as `varied()` with a mean of the value
predicts.

### Partisans: the guerrilla on a building's death (for `partisans`, WoL #114, 2026-11-27)

Evidence: REA `ev_24ea7f00…` (xref of `L"Partisans"`), `ev_314c4bc8…` / `ev_96a4691a…` (xrefs of `L"PartisanType"` / `L"PartisanCount"`), `ev_f4382097…` (FUN_004B4EBD), `ev_51f265eb…` (FUN_009C07D6 pseudocode), bundle `rea/evidence/age3y-b1a1dab7-partisans-20261127.json`; the rest `cs` (capstone over the stock exe). [O] observed, [I] inferred, [U] unknown.

| What | Where | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Civ tag `<Partisans>` | civs.xml parser, the name compare at 0x52AB43 (`L"Partisans"` 0xBCF69C), body 0x6599F1 | confirmed (`cs`, `ev_24ea7f00…`) | [O] A yes/no flag, not a unit name: the value goes through the integer read 0x59B526 → 0x59A71D (a scanf with format 0xBA282C) into a local preset to 1 (0x6599FB). Value 1 sets bit 4 of the civ flag bytes, `or byte [[civ+128h]],10h` (0x659A14); any other number clears it (0x659A26). [O] A text that is not a number (WoL's Tupinamba `UybassyArcher`) fails the scan, the local keeps 1, so it reads as `1`: the unit name is ignored. [O] A civ with no tag: the bit stays 0 (the civ init clears bits 0 and 1 at 0x52A5EF and never sets bit 4). |
| Proto tag `<PartisanType>` | handler 0xA129D3 (registered at 0x51D633 in the proto tag table, `L"PartisanType"` 0xBCD9F8) | confirmed (`cs`, `ev_314c4bc8…`) | [O] One slot: `mov [proto+160h],eax` (0xA12A90), the proto ID by name (0x5A2346, then a case-blind walk of the name list `[db+190h]`). A second `<PartisanType>` in the same unit overwrites the first; there is no list. Empty text: error 0xEB5; an unknown name: error 0xEB6, the tag ignored (the slot keeps what it had). [I] Names are known before the tags are read (forward references such as SerbianInferno, unit 3268, from TownCenter, unit 304, resolve like `<DeadReplacement>`), so the last line wins: WoL's TownCenter, ArtilleryDepot and Market (UybassyArcher, then SerbianInferno) keep SerbianInferno for every civ. **Confirmed in game (2026-11-27):** a Serbian Town Center gave `6 x SerbianInferno`, read from its copy's `+160h`. |
| Proto tag `<PartisanCount>` | handler 0xA114C1 (registered at 0x51D64C, `L"PartisanCount"` 0xBCD9E8) | confirmed (`cs`, `ev_96a4691a…`) | [O] One int, `mov [proto+164h],edx` (0xA1151B); empty: error 0xEA1, not a number: 0xEA2. |
| Defaults | proto ctor 0x5F7394, 0x5F75B9 / 0x5F75BF | confirmed (`cs`) | [O] `+160h` from EAX (−1 there, [I]) and `+164h` from EDI (0). |
| Per-player copy | proto copy 0x67E011, 0x67E5A3..0x67E5B5 | confirmed (`cs`) | [O] Copies `+160h` and `+164h`, so each player's copy has its own type and count: a Data effect that writes the player's copy changes only that player. No stock tech subtype writes them (the only xrefs of the two names are the proto tag table). |
| Civ test | FUN_004B4EBD, `bool thiscall(player)` | confirmed (`cs`, `ev_f4382097…`) | [O] Walks the player's civ IDs `[player+20h]` (count `+24h`), each civ from `[db+24h]`; true (cold 0x679593) when one has `[civ+12Ch]` > 4 and bit 4 of `[[civ+128h]]`. One caller, 0x4B5332. |
| Where the spawn is decided | the kill handler FUN_004B4F70, 0x4B532F..0x4B533F, cold block 0x6A3F04..0x6A3F36 | confirmed (`cs`) | [O] `mov ecx,[esi+44h] / call 4B4EBDh / cmp al,1 / je 6A3F04h` (16 B, 0x4B532F; no branch enters 0x4B5330..0x4B533E). At 0x6A3F04: `[[unit+5Ch]+164h]` > 0 (0x6A3F07); the kill handler's first byte argument `[esp+0A0h]` == 0 (0x6A3F14; 1 for a trigger's kill, see the kill queue row; other sources not traced [U]); 0x44DDB4(unit) true (no build record `[unit+68h]`, or its progress past the threshold: a finished building); then `call 9C07D6h` (0x6A3F31) and back to 0x4B533F. ## Rendering, threads and timing (2026-10-28, for the performance investigation)

Full write-up and evidence: [performance.md](performance.md). REA bundles:
`rea/evidence/age3y-b1a1dab7/bundle-perf-20261028.json`, and `bundle-perf-20261028-d.json` for the frame
structure, world update and file reader rows. Those rows are static readings confirmed by stack sampling (§9 of
performance.md).

| VA | Role | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| 0x5AA825 | Timer setup | `timeBeginPeriod(1)`; the matching `timeEndPeriod(1)` is in the shutdown function FUN_0057D028. |
| 0x455D91 | Sim update length | Single-player step = real elapsed time, capped at 100 ms × game speed. `fixedUpdate` / `RunAsFastAsPossible` / `MinRandUpdate` branches. |
| 0x54EF1E | Renderer ctor | Sets PresentationInterval `[+638h]` = IMMEDIATE and calls 0x54EDAA. |
| 0x54E60B | Renderer init | `Direct3DCreate9(0x20)` → `[+488h]`; adapter `[+640h]` from cfg `d3dadapter`. |
| 0x545BB5 | Create/reset device | Present params at `+604h`: BackBufferCount 1, DISCARD. BehaviorFlags `(MIXED 80h or SOFTWARE 20h) \| MULTITHREADED 4`. CreateDevice at 0x545D78; device at `+4D0h`. |
| 0x54EDAA | Skinning MT gate | MT = `!singleThreadedBatcher && cpus > 1` → `[renderer+9290h]`, through 0x54ED40 on batchers at `+8EE4h` / `+9294h`. |
| 0x5D6B8A | Skinning worker start | Creates the single worker 0x4058BF (event 0xC674B8, CS 0xC674C8). Called once from 0x5D6B53 when `[renderer+9290h]` is set. |
| 0x40B65E | Deform (producer) | Queues a job (cap 0x100, scratch cap 0x5000 `__m128`) and calls `SetEvent`, or deforms synchronously. |
| 0x4058BF | Skinning worker | Takes jobs under the CS while `[0xC674C0]`; done count `[0xC67500]`; never told to quit. |
| 0x40599C | Queue flush | Drains on the calling thread with `[0xC674C0] = 0`, spins until the worker finishes, resets the queue. |
| 0x40B54F | Run one job | `thiscall(deformer, job*)` → `deformerdlly.dll` SSE exports by vertex format. |
| 0x5734E0 | Register cfg variable | `thiscall(0xC66500, name, desc, default?, 0)` → ID. |
| 0x4020C3 | Cfg "is defined" | `thiscall(0xC66500, id)`. |
| 0x404EE0 | Main loop (WinMain body) | Message pump 0x402EF7, then `[0xC66234]->vtable[74h]` through the `jmp eax` stub 0x406A01 (returns to 0x404FAD). Bundle `-d`, `ev_bbbe9b38…`. |
| 0x40542F / 0x405523 / 0x405574 | Per-frame function (game vtable+74h), cut by Ghidra at the `timeGetTime` stubs 0x405510 / 0x405561 | Update = mode manager 0x405248; render = `vtable[78h]` through 0x408FE4 (returns to 0x4056B1). The game's own per-frame ms averages are at `[game+CC0h]` (update) and `[game+CBCh]` (render), refreshed every second. `ev_0cf19746…`, `ev_66b6966f…`, `ev_fa2d724b…`. |
| 0x405248 | Mode manager update | Mode stack ("Mode to be terminated is: %d…"); updates each active mode through 0x405339. |
| 0x44E0CD (+0x44E183, 0x48FFD9, 0x48FFF3) | Game-mode update, in pieces | 0x44E183 is the only caller of 0x455D91. 0x48FFF3 calls the world update at 0x49000B. `ev_ee973830…`, `ev_ad8fe973…`. |
| 0x481B1C | World update | `this` = `[game+13Ch]`; assert `.\world.cpp`. Phases are counted in `[world+810h]` and separated by 0x491C94; the sim RNG is at `[0xC6658C]+18h`. `ev_dd5f48b8…`. |
| 0x4848BB | Unit update loop | Over `[world+D4h/D8h]`, `[+E0h/E4h]` and `[+ECh/F0h]`; per-unit update 0x482F50. `ev_4677dfc3…`. |
| 0x45084C / 0x450A4E / 0x44FC96 | Render pass dispatcher / main view pass / world draw | 0x44FC96 builds the visible-unit lists and draws each unit through 0x452392. Other passes call it from 0x4597A4. `ev_cf045b59…`. |
| 0x408399 | Render (game `vtable[78h]`) | Particles 0x408AAB, world 0x40B9BA, UI 0x41C6D3, Present wrapper 0x41CA31, then `_control87(0x9001F, 0xFFFFF)`. Probe times per call site in [performance.md](performance.md) §11. `ev_91eb3a47…` (perf-20261110). |
| 0x40B9BA (+0x40BAFE) | World render | 0x40BAFE is its tail (`jmp` at 0x40BAB3). Calls 0x40BCF0(0) and 0x40BCF0(1): `vtable[14h]` of each view with the pass index; the world view is 0x45084C (pass 0 → 0x459719 → 0x4597A4, pass 1 → 0x450A4E). `ev_89f7c631…`, `ev_13da66a1…`, `ev_faaa8ead…`. |
| 0x408AAB | Particle update | Walks the emitters, updates them (0x408E36 → 0x425018 → 0x424A9B / 0x424E33) and sorts them by distance (0x4087F3). Visual state only. `ev_084ed1cb…`, `ev_d8d52214…`. |
| 0x40D25B | Cloth flag draw | `thiscall(flag manager [0xC67364], byte frontOnly, byte lowDetail)`, `ret 8`; callers 0x450684 (world draw 0x44FC96, after the batch flushes) and 0x429429 (0x42922A), both after the cfg test of `[0xC67338]`. Sets render state 16h (`D3DRS_CULLMODE`) through 0x4203AB to 3 (CCW), or to 1 (none) with `frontOnly`, and restores it at the end. Then per queued flag (`[mgr+68h]`: count, `+10h` data, 34h bytes each: `+0` the index of its record in `[mgr+24h]`, `+4` passed to 0x40BEA5; the record: `+0Ch` source buffer index, `+10h` columns, `+14h` rows, `+1Ch/+20h/+24h` U scale, V scale, V offset, `+2Ch` a byte that picks the shader `[mgr+7Ch]` with `pixelxformColor` (0xBE0974) or `[mgr+80h]` with `blendColor` (0xB9A914), set from `+30h` through 0x405C87) it fills vertices when the grid or the source buffer differs from the previous flag's, and draws through 0x40D039 (indices from 0x42042E). The queue's count is zeroed at the end. It is the cloth flags' draw, not "shader/material parameters" (the reading of [performance.md](performance.md) §8, from those two strings). REA `ev_20b69500…`, `ev_f342b619…` (fastvertexfill-20261110). |
| 0x40D523..0x40D654 | Cloth flag vertex fill | Two loops over rows `[esp+14h]` and columns `[esp+18h]`: source vertex `[mgr+3Ch+index*4] + ([mgr+14h]*row + column)*20h` (position, normal, U, V) → front vertex at EAX−10h (U × `[esp+50h]`, V × `[esp+54h]` − `[esp+58h]`), then at 0x40D5C0..0x40D5FD the back vertex at EDX−10h, read back from the front one, normal × −1.0 (`[0xB856B8]`); skipped with `frontOnly` (`[esp+70h]`). `fastVertexFill` replaces them. |
| 0x40A6A2 / 0x40A5B1 | Dynamic vertex buffer: take a piece | 0x40A6A2 `thiscall(renderer, count, stride, int* first)`, `ret 0Ch`: aligns `used` to the stride and calls 0x40A5B1(`[renderer+660h]`, bytes, &offset), which locks `{buffer, size, used, byte reset}` at `used` with flags 0x1800 (`NOOVERWRITE \| NOSYSLOCK`), or from 0 with 0x2800 (`DISCARD \| NOSYSLOCK`) when the piece does not fit or `reset` is set, and writes a zero to the first and last byte under SEH. Returns the pointer; the caller unlocks (`[buffer]->vtable[30h]`). `cs`; REA `ev_9e4dad98…`. |
| 0x5AE756 | Dynamic vertex buffer: create | When `[renderer+660h]` is null: size 0x200000 (or a cfg value, the lookup 0x46A144 of the ID at `[[0xD6FBC8]]`), `device->CreateVertexBuffer(size, 0x208, 0, 0, &record[0], 0)` at 0x5AE7D5: `D3DUSAGE_DYNAMIC \| D3DUSAGE_WRITEONLY`, FVF 0, `D3DPOOL_DEFAULT`. `cs`; REA `ev_4b205dae…`. At runtime (2026-11-10, `fastVertexFill verify`): `GetDesc` gives usage 0x208, pool 0, 2,097,152 bytes, and the lock points into private memory with protection 0x404 (`PAGE_READWRITE \| PAGE_WRITECOMBINE`). |
| 0x48241C | Unit bounding box | `thiscall(unit)`: rebuilds `[unit+B4h]+34h…+74h` from the model bounds and orientation through 0x422DC6; calls `_control87(0x9001F)`. Reached by the simulation and, through 0x4592FE (which clears dirty bit `[[unit+4]+5]` & 8 and sets bit 5 of `[[unit+4]+1]`), by the render. `ev_de03df52…`; writes seen with `trace_writes.py` (2026-11-10). |
| 0x452D7B…0x452DC5 | Per-unit spring (render) | In 0x452B8E under the per-unit draw: damped spring on `[unit+B4h]+DCh/+E0h` with the frame time. Zeroed by the simulation at 0x47253F (0x4722E6). Hardware write breakpoint, 2026-11-10. |
| 0x468450 | File stream open | Checks for the `l33t` compressed header (inflate stream, vtable 0xBA3318); otherwise uses a raw stream. `ev_bb0bb32c…`. |
| 0x4683F4 | Raw file stream read | One `ReadFile` per call, no buffering. 0x599121 reads 4 bytes through 0x5990D1; 0x43B8FF reads a 16-byte header as four such reads. `ev_1c4e2090…`. |
| 0x55A185 | Single-instance check | `CreateSemaphoreW(NULL, 1, 1, name)` → `[this+880h]`, then `WaitForSingleObject(h, 0)`; a timeout (0x102) jumps to 0x6D1039. The name is formatted as `Global\%s` (0xBE3768) when `[esp+18h]` is 2 (inferred: the NT platform ID), so it is shared by every copy of the game in every session. Observed 2026-10-28: the name is `Global\Age3` (logged by `multipleInstances`), and a second copy started from another folder stops at "Sólo puede ejecutarse una copia del juego al mismo tiempo"; that 0x6D1039 leads to that dialog is inferred. `multipleInstances` renames this one semaphore per folder. Capstone listing (not REA): `rea/evidence/age3y-b1a1dab7/single-instance/`. |

### Last in line and the XS runtime (2026-11-11, WoL's stutters)

What runs in WoL's long frames ([performance.md](performance.md) §9, "What WoL's stutters are"). REA bundle
`rea/evidence/age3y-b1a1dab7/wol-stutter-20261111.json` (Ghidra 12.1.4, read only: no names written to the
database); capstone listings for the rest.

| VA | Role | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0x4918A8 (+ cold chunk 0x68FB0C…0x68FC51, which Ghidra lists as FUN_0068FB04) | **LastInLine recompute** | confirmed | `thiscall(tree, Player*)`, `ret 4`. Runs only when the player is not null and `[tree+11h]` is set; clears it. For each entry i of the tree: bit 22 (ForceLastInLine) set → set bit 16; else scan every other entry j (0…count−1, the template record when j's entry is null) for one whose definition has a prerequisite "TechStatus active i" (0x94BC1D) and that the player can obtain (0x4C5725); found → clear bit 16, none → set bit 16 unless bit 32 (YPNeverLastInLine). Then, for the local player (`[game+3C0h]`), `[[game+920h]]->vtable[4]` (a UI refresh). Cost: count × (count + prerequisite entries) checks. `ev_011dfe82…`. Callers 0x491A39 (in the per-player loop of 0x491A40) and the forced update 0x852850 (`ev_47eb4e7d…`). |
| 0x94BC1D | Definition has "TechStatus active <id>" | confirmed | `bool thiscall(entry, techId)`, `ret 4`: over the definition's prerequisites (`[def+1Ch]`, byte length `[def+20h]`, item getter 0x4C5DBD): type byte `+5` = 1, status byte `+0Ch` = 2 (active), `+8` = the tech ID. Read only. `ev_011dfe82…`. |
| 0x4C5725 | Can the player obtain the tech | confirmed | `bool thiscall(Player*, techId)`, `ret 4`. Reads the entry's bits 4 and 28 with `[player+50Ah]` and `[player+50h]`, the list `[player+3B4h]` (0x447F3C), then the civ lists through 0x4C5DA9 / 0x4C5CA1 / 0x4C5D2E / 0x4C5AFB. A capstone walk of it and of every callee down to the leaves finds **no memory write** except the stack. `ev_22d59ab1…`. |
| 0x94AE8C | Set LastInLine dirty | confirmed | `thiscall(root = [world+7Ch], playerId, bool)`, `ret 8`: writes `[tree+11h]`. Callers (`ev_d652dac7…`): 0x94CF27 (activate), 0x94C33A (set obtainable), 0x94CDB3 (set unobtainable), each only for a tech with bit 18 (HomeCity); and 0x8D002C, 0x8D554F, 0x8DCFC0 (not read). |
| 0x506A41 | Set the tree's `+10h` dirty byte | confirmed | Same shape as 0x94AE8C (`ev_2f47b251…`); see "Tech prerequisites" above. |
| 0x94CF27 | Activate a tech for a player | confirmed | `thiscall(entry, Player*, …, bool* out, …)`. Unless already active (bits 18/29 and the research count `[entry+21h]` against `[def+32h]` decide), sets bits 0, 1, 5, 6, the time `[entry+1Ch]`, 0x94C285(1.0), 0x506A41, 0x94AE8C for a HomeCity tech, bumps `[entry+21h]`, applies the effects through 0x94DAE1 → 0x7F7E97, sends the "researched" message unless the tech is Shadow (bit 11) or HomeCity (bit 18). This is the "recursive evaluator" §9 named in 2026-10: a tech's effects reach other techs and units, so it nests. `ev_91f91cee…`. |
| 0x94C33A / 0x94CDB3 | Set a tech obtainable / unobtainable | confirmed | Write bit 0, clear bits 1 and 3, set bit 6, then 0x506A41, and 0x94AE8C for a HomeCity tech. `ev_d6296011…`. |
| 0x852850 | Forced tree update | confirmed | Sets `[tree+10h]` and `[tree+11h]`, then 0x4919CE and 0x4918A8. `ev_d6296011…`. |
| Tech flag bits | parser 0x622B7D (returns at 0x6B11DB…, 0x622F80, 0x6232CE/DC) | confirmed (capstone) | The flag name gives the bit index in the entry's bitset: Volatile 2, AgeUpgrade 4, DynamicCost 7, AlwaysShowButton 8, HideFromDetailHelp 10, Shadow 11, UniqueProtoUnitInstance 13, UpgradeTech 17, HomeCity 18, TeamTech 19, OrPrereqs 21, ForceLastInLine 22, UniqueTech 23, CountsTowardEconomicScore 24, CountsTowardMilitaryScore 25, DoNotQueue 26, NativeDance 27, RevoltTech 28, YPInfiniteTech 29, CheckLandHCGatherPoint 30, CheckWaterHCGatherPoint 31, YPNeverLastInLine 32, YPConsulateTech 40. Bit 16 is not a flag: it is the computed LastInLine. |
| Readers of bit 16 | 0x69672C, 0x9AD72F, 0x9B757A | confirmed (capstone), not complete | Each `push 10h; call 0x44D863` (the entry's bit test) inside the command-panel builders 0x695BB6, 0x9ACAF4, 0x9B687E (strings `trainInSelectedByID`, `researchByID`, `uiBuildMode`). No `cmp [r+8],10h` + byte test of bit 16 elsewhere in `.text`. **Inference:** a UI flag (which upgrade button to show); other access patterns were not searched. |
| 0x67A1FD | Player update | confirmed | Asserts `.\player.cpp`; calls the AI update at 0x67A6D4. Reached from the world update 0x481CE9 → 0x490360 → 0x490960. |
| 0x720CA7 | AI update (`BAI::update`) | confirmed | String `P#%d (%s) BAI::update:`; calls `BXSRuntime::interpretRules` at 0x721203. |
| 0x428E1F | `BXSRuntime::interpretRules` | confirmed | `thiscall(runtime, int context, int currentTime, int timeLimit, …)`; data = `[[runtime+30h]+4·context]`; logs `BXSRuntime::interpretRules: currentTime=%d, timeLimit=%d:` when the byte `[[runtime+10h]+1Fh]` is 1; sets the global 0xC665F0 = `[runtime+4]` while it runs; runs 0x423B3D(`[runtime+20h]`, data, time, limit, &out) until the rules are done. `ev_8dc255be…`. |
| 0x42CF3F / 0x42CEED / 0x42B496 | XS call a function / evaluate loop / one evaluation step | confirmed | 0x42CF3F sets up a function on the data's stack (errors "invalid function entry", "failed to setup function on stack"); 0x42CEED steps 0x42B496 while `[data+E0h]` > 0. The data's XS call stack: `[data+DCh]` entries of 18h bytes, count `[data+E0h]`, entry `+0` the function ID. The interpreter's strings ("evaluateCode PC=%d", "Enter '%s' function.", "Infinite recursion limit") are in the chunks around 0x42B990…0x42CBCA. `ev_8dc255be…`. |
| 0x42B45A | XS function name | confirmed | `thiscall(program, id)`: `*[[program+30h]+4·id]`, count `[program+34h]`. `ev_2b868d6a…`. |

After "Invalid bone name \"%s\" in part declaration for model \"%s\"" (0xC0B878) it calls the loader on the loaded model at 0x658C35: a reload while in use (6 times in the traced game). Only when `bl` is set (capstone, 2026-11-23): the bone name was not in the global bone name table 0xD6F478 (lookup 0x58E506 returned -1 at 0x4F0224) and was added to it (0x59C213 at 0x658B6A); 0x4F010C then did not find it in the model (0x4F0240, `jle` 0x658BA4, the error). So a model is reloaded once per bone name new to the session. Seen 2026-11-23: model `war of the triple alliance\dlc\other\new trees\cypressa`, bone `oak a death` (WoL's `cypress.xml` gives the cypress trees `terrain\trees\Damage\tree_oak_a.dmg`); the game crashed 12 s later in `granny2.dll+0x2C8CD` from the world draw (0x44EA8C, 0x451446). Callers on that path: 0x4F07E9, 0x4F0974, 0x4F0AA5 (the damage template's parts). 2026-11-23 (agent M, REA `ev_5cd8bb32…` again, capstone): the reload exists because a model's id-to-skeleton map (`0x4F010C` → `[model+10h]`, lookup `0x484329` → `0x40722F`) is built at load from the names then in the table; with `bl` clear the game takes no reload and keeps the part (bone id set, no bone in that model), continuing at 0x658C3A → 0x4F0246 either way. `dmgPartBoneFix` makes `je 658C3Ah` (0x658C1A) a `jmp`. The bone table is a growable array (0x59C213 resizes through 0x59C1E6), and its name compare (0x4015C5) is by length, then text. The other adder in this area, 0x658D70 (FUN_004F0540's bone lookup), returns to 0x4F0595 without a reload. |
| Bone name table | 0xD6F478 (lookup FUN_0058E506, add FUN_0059C213) | capstone (2026-11-23) | `[table+4]` the entries (8 B each), `[table+8]` the count; 0x59C213 returns the existing index when the name is there, else appends. Callers of the add: 0x43B6B6, 0x59C10A, 0x658B6A (part declaration), 0x658D70, 0x6E6312, 0x8059E7, 0x8C4AC8; which one reads `<definebone>` is not checked (inferred: an anim file's `<definebone>` adds its name, as the error text says). |

## Turrets, facing and the Granny pose (2026-11-24, WoL #102, the gatling camel's gun)

Evidence: `rea/evidence/gcamel-turret-20261124.json`; capstone sweeps of `.text`; a read-breakpoint trace in game
(`trace_writes.py --rw`, WoLReal7, 45 s of a GatlingCamel firing at 30 wagons).

| Name | Address | Status | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `<Turret>` of a tactics action | handler 0x68E0AF (tag compare at 0x5F9D7A, in FUN_0068DE73) | confirmed: parsed, never read | The text (int) → `[action+94h]`; `tracking="always"` sets bit 25 of the flag bytes (`or byte [[action+64h]+3],2`, 0x68E0FC); `maxTurnAnglePerSecond` × 0.0174533 → float `[action+98h]`. In game (`<turret tracking="always" maxTurnAnglePerSecond="123.5">7777</turret>` on GatlingCamelSaddle's CannonAttack) the three values were in memory (7777, 2.1555, flags 0x82500403), the camel still turned whole to each target, and read breakpoints on `+94h` and `+98h` saw no read in 45 s while `+34h` (ROF) was read 79 times. No instruction tests bit 25 (the other flag bits are tested as `cmp [action+68h],N` and a byte test; none with 19h). Dead data. |
| `BeginTurretRotation` | name table entry 26h, registered at 0x5249F9 (`[esi+2000h]`, beside `DestroyUnitType` 25h and `TargetDiedWall` 27h) | observed (name only) | No sender found; the attack action's event switch (0x48CF5A) covers types 5..20h only. |
| Face a point | FUN_0048AA9F, `bool thiscall(unit, Vector* point)`, `ret 4`; by unit ID FUN_0048AA5D | confirmed (capstone, REA `ev_9821b6b6…`) | Gate: the unit's vtable `+8Ch` (through 0x4B2834 `jmp eax`; false → 0x494686). Takes the horizontal direction to the point, normalised, and calls FUN_0048AB4A(dir), which stores it as the wanted forward at `[[unit+4Ch]+18h]` (skipped when equal); returns 1. Callers of 0x48AA9F: the attack 0x48A7ED, 0x48A948, 0x48A990; 0x4ED86C, 0x4F6F9A, 0x4F6FB5, 0x8E1E3B, 0x8E2131, 0x9D413F, 0x9E7F1B; 21 of 0x48AA5D. |
| Attack update and facing | FUN_0048A330 (attack unit action: `[ua+4]` tactics action, `[ua+10h]` unit, `[ua+14h]` target ID, `[ua+61h]` state) | confirmed (REA `ev_f67127f1…`) | State 0: in range, 0x48AA5D(target) (or the point form) is called and the attack starts (state 1) at once: no facing tolerance, the turn happens while it fires. State 1 calls it again on every update. In game the forward vector snapped to the target between two 0.5 s samples. |
| Proto RotateInPlace / ConstrainOrientation | `[proto+50h]` flag bytes: byte 10h bit 1 (0x5FCCCE), byte 6 bit 7 (0x5F8E60) | capstone | `TurnRate` is read by 0x5F7FCB and stored through a virtual setter (not followed). |
| `TurnRate` and the body's turn | `[proto+94h]` float, radians a second; the wanted forward `[[unit+4Ch]+18h]`, the turning byte `[[unit+4Ch]+24h]` | confirmed (memory and log in game, 2026-11-27, WoLReal4: MEZamburak and GatlingCamelDeployed) | [O] `[proto+94h]` read 1.75 and 3.0 after `<TurnRate>` 1.75 / 3.0 in protoy. The forward turns at a constant rate (no easing near the end): 18 gave 90 degrees in about 95 ms, 3.0 gave 180 in 1063 ms (169/s), 1.75 gave 180 in about 1.8 s (100/s). [O] `[unit+20h]` changes on every drawn frame of a turn (the simulation steps each frame here), so the model is drawn from it with no interpolation. |
| The attack update's turn checks | state 0 after the face call: 0x4A8619 (turning byte, then bit 14h of the unit action's own flag bits `[ua+30h]`, count `[ua+34h]`); state 1 after face C: 0x4B3347 (turning byte and `[proto+94h]` < 5.0 at 0xBA0AF8) -> 0x48BFC7 | confirmed (capstone); [O] in game | [O] With `TurnRate` 1.75 and 3.0 both camels **fired while they turned** (shots logged at the arc's edge, yaw 30, mid-turn), and a tracer saw 0x48BFC7 never hit: the check at 0x4B3347 is passed once per attack cycle (two hits in a minute), while the shots come from the action's animation path (0x48F88D, the launch, from the action list update 0x485E30) without a facing test. So no stock data stops a camel firing while it turns. |
| Attack unit action state | `[ua+61h]` byte; the switch at 0x48A65A (`movzx eax,byte [esi+61h] / cmp eax,16h / ja 48A728h`, jump table 0x48A9B8 via bytes 0x48A9D8) in FUN_0048A330 | confirmed (capstone; memory in game) | [O] GatlingCamelDeployed firing at a House, sampled every 5 ms: state 1 while it fires (a burst of six launches about 170 ms apart), 21 for 3 s (the reload), 1 again; 25 when the target dies, then no attack action. [O] The update passes the switch on every update. turretArc holds the attack there (0x48A65A) during a shown turn: in state 1 it leaves through 0x48BFC7 (the stock slow turner's wait: `[ua]` vtable +34h with 0, i.e. back to state 0, then the update's epilogue `pop edi / pop esi / pop ebx / add esp,8 / ret`, the same frame as the switch). In game: 0 shots in 12 shown turns of 90 and 180 degrees, the first shot 0.4 to 1.3 s after each. |
| Action manager's attack timer | `[[unit+58h]+0B8h]` game ms, `[[unit+58h]+0BCh]` ms | confirmed (memory in game) | [O] +0B8h is the time of the last launch (it changes at every shot of a burst), +0BCh a wait that falls by about 170 ms from shot to shot (3918, 3750, ... 3078 for the gatling; 4098 for the zamburak, whose +0B8h moves 5002 ms a cycle). The state 0 path allows an attack when their sum is at most now + 10 (0x4A85CB). [O] Raising them did not stop a burst already under way (state 1 does not read them): not a way to hold an attack. |
| Granny sampling | IAT 0xB847AC `GrannySampleModelAnimations`, 0xB847B0 `GrannyBuildWorldPose`, 0xB847A4 `GrannyGetWorldPoseComposite4x4Array`, 0xB8473C `GrannyGetWorldPose4x4Array` | confirmed (import table, capstone) | Four sample sites, each `(modelInstance = [visual+24h], 0, boneCount, localPose = [[0xC67318]+8])` then BuildWorldPose into `[[0xC67318]+10h]`: 0x40768B (the bone frame getter's path, 0x407272 → 0x4073F9), 0x4DB727, 0xADCC34 and 0xADCE4F (render). The local pose between the two calls is where a bone can be turned for every later use (attachments read bone frames through 0x407272). |
| Tag lookup in an animation's tag file | FUN_004A9751, thiscall(anim file, ...) (callers 0x48EDFF, 0x48EEAB, 0x4A98B9 (0x4A986F, the visual's wrapper), 0xADD9FE) | capstone | Reads the `.gr2.precomp` tracks (`[file+28h]`, `+34h`, 12-byte keys, duration `[file+58h]`). The callers at 0x48EDFF / 0x48EEAB look up the tags whose IDs sit beside `MASTER` and `R HAND` in the table filled at 0x5210F8 (0xDC147C, 0xDC149C): not the launch point (corrected 2026-11-24). |
| The projectile launch | FUN_00499422, thiscall(attack unit action, unit, target, ..., launch data), called at 0x48F88D | confirmed (REA `ev_0a3ab214…`, capstone, in game) | The start of the shot: from the launch data `[arg5+38h..40h]` (0x4CEAF4), else the launch points of attach type `[0xD6F680]` (0x484304, then 0x45F22D world, the nearest to the target, 0x4AB4F8..0x4AB5D4), else the unit's position; all meet at 0x4995D5 with the start (world) at `[esp+1Ch]`, the aim point at `[esp+28h]`, EBX the unit action, EDI the unit, then the direction and FUN_00498ACC (the projectile). Seen in game (turretArc log): the gatling camel's start sat 0.24 m from its centre and turned about the centre with the direction to the target, not with the unit's facing. |
| A unit's visual and its parts | `[unit+60h]` visual: `+1Ch` its model part (vtable 0xB964A0, its Granny model instance at `+24h`), `+24h` its attachments (an array of visuals of the same layout), `+28h` their count | confirmed (memory, in game) | The gatling camel: the camel's part; attachments the rider (whose own attachment is his hat) and the gun. A part's `+0B0h` is not a sibling list (it held another part once and 1 another time). |
| Granny skeleton in this granny2.dll | `+0` name, `+4` bone count, `+8` bones, 156 B each (`+0` the name) | confirmed (memory, in game) | `GrannyFindBoneByName` returned true with index 46 for a name a 46-bone skeleton lacks: check the index and the name. `GrannyGetLocalPoseTransform`, the control and animation-binding exports are all exported (not imported by the game). |
